# 14 — Execution tracker

**The only tracker.** Ids are stable and never reused. Status: `Open`, `In progress`, `Blocked`, `Paused`, `Done`, as defined
with Definition of Done in [05](05-PHASES-AND-TASKS.md). Phase state and waivers are in
[06](06-ROADMAP-AND-DELIVERY.md). Severity: P0 blocks a phase gate or is a security or evidence defect; P1 blocks a
slice; P2 otherwise.

Docs are true as of `main` at `900d0b6` (2026-11-17, PR #16 merged; its CI run 44018844840 green on every job), with pull request #18 open on `sprint1-stabilization`; merge steps update their own rows. A row is `Done` only when its gate is met and a CI run on `main` shows it; work proven only on a branch stays `In progress` with what is pending.

## Owner decisions

| DEC-01 | Product name | Done | Owner, 2026-11-10 (instruction 4): **OpenSeal**. Chosen because "OpenSign" is OpenSign Labs' AGPL e-signature product ([52](52-COMPETITOR-STUDY.md)). A web search found only an unrelated, old .NET library called openSEAL on SourceForge; trademark search remains `DEC-07`. Crates stay `casual-sign-*`; the local folder `opensign/` may be renamed to `openseal/` when the repo is created | Avoids confusion and a trademark dispute |
| DEC-02 | Where to build; how to share code with the sisters | Done | Owner, 2026-11-11 (instruction 12), as recommended: Separate repo in this folder; copy from OpenCalc and OpenPresent with `PROVENANCE.md` (as OpenPresent's `DEC-02`) | Sets ADR-003 |

| DEC-04 | Acrobat verification | Done | Owner, 2026-11-11 (instruction 12), as recommended: Release gate `PV-01`, run in a CI container or on a machine that is not the owner's; phases close on our verifier + pyHanko + EU DSS | So work never waits on Acrobat; "valid in Acrobat" stays unclaimable until then |

Built 2026-11-13: migration `0028_quota_enforcement.sql`: `openseal_consume_quota` (ledger row, then conditional updates `value + n <= limit` of the organization counter and then the workspace counter in one subtransaction; a refusal rolls back all three; a source counted before answers `replayed` before any check; a suspended organization refuses), `openseal_quota_gauge_lock`/`_check` for `seats` and `storage_bytes` (organization then workspace counter row locked before the change, refusal only when the change raised the gauge past a `hard` limit), `quota_notices` (organization table, append-only: `soft` records each action over the limit with its overage, `notify` the crossing), `openseal_meter_apply` now moves the organization row first (one lock order). `casual-sign-store` `TenantTx::consume_quota`, `gauge_lock`, `gauge_check` (limits from `plans::effective` in the same transaction; no plan, no quota), `StoreError::Plan`; `envelopes_sent` enforced in `casual-sign-host::apply` on `envelope.sent` (every send path); seats on `POST /users` and `PATCH /users/{id}` (profile, enable); storage on envelope and template document uploads; problem details carry `meter`, `limit`, `used`, `period_end`. Codes `CS-PLAN-0002` to `0005` issued. Seen red: `casual-sign-store/tests/quota.rs::parallel_sends_in_two_workspaces_never_pass_the_organization_quota` (`SEC-U01`: 50 sends, limit 10, two workspaces; 17 passed with check-then-update), `parallel_member_additions_never_pass_the_seat_limit` (refusal ignored: 12 of 12 passed), `casual-sign-worker/tests/plan_enforcement.rs::a_sent_envelope_completes_with_a_verified_seal_after_quota_suspension_and_archive` (`SEC-U02`: storage enforced on the worker's sealed version, the seal job failed), `casual-sign-server/tests/quota.rs` (seat and storage refusals ignored: 201; review added `a_send_past_the_envelope_quota_is_refused_and_the_draft_stays_a_draft`, 200 with `record_usage` in place of `consume_quota`, 403 `CS-PLAN-0002` with it). | E2E-02 | GitHub CI `e2e` failed on `main` at the v0.0.1 docs commit (run 43129570251): WebKit `guided-ceremony.spec.mjs:217` "review lists every value…" fails its axe scan on the review step; other browsers pass. Find the violation, fix it, or show it flaky and fix the wait | Done | P1 | Found 2026-11-12 by the main session. Cause (run's trace): the review's entrance fade (`.os-sign__screen`, 220 ms) was at its first frames when axe measured, though the animation wait just before had seen nothing running; the 15 nodes are the whole screen at about 2 % opacity, the shape a scan taken mid-fade gives. Fix: `expectAccessible` (e2e/helpers.mjs) takes the scan again after the animations settle until it is clean (15 s, which covers a slow scan); a violation of the settled page still fails. New spec "the accessibility scan is taken again when it caught a view fading in" holds the fade and was red without the fix, green with it; no web app change. Proven on the next GitHub CI runs on main **Done 2026-11-14 (tracker close-out):** WebKit `guided-ceremony.spec.mjs:241` (the spec once at line 217) and the re-scan spec at line 227 passed in GitHub CI `e2e` on eight consecutive completed runs on main: 43461637023, 43469582398, 43474417057, 43481106364, 43482114678, 43492019831, 43499151699, 43503843137. | `e2e` green on WebKit for that spec on three consecutive runs |

A code in a log would be a `SEC-A` defect, so P0 until the cause is named 2026-11-17: the test passed in `server-db` on `main` run 44001061169 (both attempts); the cause is still not named, so the gate is not met. 2026-11-18, cause named: **a chance match, not a leak.** At `09cac8d` the check was `logged.contains(code)` over the test's `tracing` capture only (the server's `request` lines: an RFC 3339 time with exactly six fractional digits, `…:33.246944Z`, a UUIDv7 `request_id`, method, route template, status, ms; 26 lines and 37 to 85 six-digit windows in a local run). No field of those lines can carry a code, so a hit could only be a code equal to a timestamp's microseconds or a digit run in an id; the capture itself was never printed, so the exact line cannot be recovered from the job log. The run's own printed security lines for this test (job log, 15 lines) hold 46 digit runs of six or more and no six digits standing alone. Found while checking: the server's security events and the worker's lines go to their own sinks, not `tracing`, so the check never saw them: a worker line carrying the code (a mutation in `worker.rs` `email_otp`) passed the old capture (`test result: ok`). Fixed: the test captures both into memory beside `tracing`, matches the code standing alone (since `7063fb5`), and a failure names the line with the code masked; with the mutation it fails `a code in the log` (`test result: FAILED`), without it passes. Done on a green `main` run | The cause named from the log; if a leak, the leak fixed with the test seen red; if a coincidence, the check made exact and seen red on a real leak |

Done 2026-11-13: the worker ends the run `failed` with the job's code in the transaction that makes an analysis job dead, whatever killed it (`Ml::fail_run_of_dead_job`); a run left behind by a dead worker (job dead by the claim, or its last attempt's lease lapsed) is ended `failed` with the job's code or `CS-WRK-0015` when the run is next read or asked for (`Ml::fail_abandoned_runs`), so the sender sees `failed`, never "reading" for good, and sending is never held. | ASVS-01 | Staff multi-factor authentication in the tenant server: TOTP (reuse `-console/src/totp.rs` and its sealed seeds) and WebAuthn (`CON-13`), mandatory for administrators and enforceable by organization policy; one-time recovery codes (112 bits, single use, hashed); the factor is required in password reset; identity proofing before a lost factor is replaced. **Built 2026-11-13 (`q-ASVS-01`):** TOTP enrolment (`POST /auth/mfa/totp/enrol`, `…/confirm`), the sign-in step (`POST /auth/mfa/verify`), ten one-time recovery codes (112 bits, stored as SHA-256, spent on use), seeds sealed AES-256-GCM under `OPENSEAL_MFA_KEY` with the user id as associated data (`-server/src/totp.rs` and `mfa.rs` copied from the console with provenance), each code single use per user (`users.totp_last_step`, migration 0032), the account policy `settings.mfa_policy` (`optional`, `administrators`, `everyone`), and a session that owes a factor refused everywhere but the sign-in steps (`403 CS-AUTH-0011`); a password reset does not bypass it (a confirmed factor is owed by every session). Web app: the code step and enrolment on the sign-in card (`webapp/signin.js`). Acceptance test `-server/tests/mfa.rs` seen red three ways (guard off; `<=` replay; recovery code not spent) and green. Review 2026-11-13: the TOTP code comparison uses `aws_lc_rs::constant_time` instead of a hand-rolled loop (`totp::tests` red with the comparison forced true), and `settings.mfa_policy: null` clears the policy like any other setting (was `422`; `tests/mfa.rs` seen red, then green). **Left:** WebAuthn (`CON-13`); `administrators` as the default policy (`ASVS-31`); replacing or resetting a lost factor with step-up and identity proofing, and regenerating recovery codes (`ASVS-32`); a QR code and a browser e2e of the step (`ASVS-33`). 2026-11-19: `CON-13` (staff) and `ASVS-32` built on a worktree branch, pending CI on `main` | In progress | P0 | Found by `SEC-ASVS-01` ([67](67-ASVS-SELF-ASSESSMENT.md)): ASVS 2.2.1, 2.3.2, 2.5.6, 2.5.7, 2.6.x, 4.3.1, 1.2.4; `SEC-A09` | An administrator without a second factor cannot reach `/api/v1`; a replayed code and a used recovery code are refused; each seen red |
